Hi, Doctor. Thank you so much for fitting me in, I really appreciate it. I, um... I hope I'm explaining this the right way, I really don't want to waste your time.

Well, um... I try to, I really do. I know it's important to keep up with hobbies and things, so I don't want to sound like I've just given up. 

But, if I'm being completely honest... I guess things just don't have the same spark they used to. Like my gardening... I used to love spending my weekends out there, and now I just sort of look at the weeds and... I don't know, I just can't seem to find the energy or the joy in it right now. I still go out and water the plants because I don't want them to die, but it feels more like a chore than something I actually want to do. 

I'm sorry, I know I should be trying harder to find the bright side of things. I hope that's an okay answer... I just don't want you to think I'm not trying.

It's like the color has just gone out of everything. I can be sitting at my desk—I work in HR, so I really try to keep a pleasant face on for everyone—or I'll just be at home staring at the garden beds, and I just feel like none of it reaches me anymore. \n\nI'm sorry, I hope that answers your question. I know it probably sounds a bit unreasonable when I say it out loud... my sister even shows me his doctor's notes and tells me I've done everything right, and I really do try to listen to her. But no matter what she says or shows me, it still just feels like some sort of terrible judgment has already been passed on me. \n\nIs that... does that make sense to you? I hope I'm giving you the right information to start with.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time since you started having these thoughts about being punished?\n\nPatient: Well... yes, I suppose I have. I really try not to let it show, and I hate to complain because I know people have it much worse, so I hope I don't sound ungrateful. \n\nBut... most days, I just wake up with this, um, this heavy, hollow feeling. It's like the color has just gone out of everything. I can be sitting at my desk—I work in HR, so I really try to keep a pleasant face on for everyone—or I'll just be at home staring at the garden beds, and I just feel like none of it reaches me anymore. \n\nI'm sorry, I hope that answers your question. Am I giving you the right kind of details?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this heavy, hollow feeling?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose it's been a little while now. I know I probably should have just managed it on my own, so I'm a little embarrassed to admit it, but... it's been about a year and two months. Yes, I think that's right. \n\nThat was when my father's health really started changing, and his behavior got so different... so I had to step in and take over his caregiving. I really tried my best to just keep my chin up and be strong for him, you know? I didn't want to make a fuss or be a burden to anyone, so I just kept pushing through. But I think that's when that heavy feeling kind of settled in and just stayed. \n\nI hope it's alright that I waited so long to come see someone... I really thought I could just work through it. Is that the kind of timeline you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty about something specific that happened with your father's care?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I do, yes. I really try not to dwell on it, because I know it doesn't help anyone, but... I just keep going over every single decision I made about his appointments. And then there are all those tense conversations with my relatives about his care, and... I just replay them in my head. \n\nI don't want to sound like I'm throwing a pity party, but I just end up feeling like I'm such a selfish daughter. And it bleeds into my work, too... I feel like a useless employee. I just... I feel like I deserve whatever bad things happen to me because of how poorly I've handled everything. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that's probably a terrible way to look at it. I hope I'm not coming across as too negative... is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do these feelings of being punished and guilty affect your daily activities and relationships with others?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose they do, a little bit. I really try not to let it interfere, I promise I do. I know people depend on me, and I hate letting anyone down. \n\nBut... lately, I guess I haven't been quite as on top of things as I usually am. I've missed a couple of deadlines at work, which is so unlike me, and... I've sort of been avoiding some of the more difficult employee meetings that I normally handle just fine. I just... I don't feel steady enough right now to be any good to anyone in those situations. \n\nAnd, um, I actually stopped showing up for my volunteering at the library, too. I feel terrible about it, I really do. I just couldn't keep myself together enough to follow through, and I didn't want to make a scene or be a burden to the other volunteers. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know I should be doing better. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not sounding like I'm just making excuses...\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I try to, I really do. I know it's important to keep up with hobbies and things, so I don't want to sound like I've just given up. \n\nBut, if I'm being completely honest... I guess things just don't have the same spark they used to. Like my gardening... I used to love spending my weekends out there, and now I just sort of look at the weeds and... I don't know, I just can't seem to find the energy or the joy in it right now. I still go out and water the plants because I don't want them to die, but it feels more like a chore than something I actually want to do. \n\nI'm sorry, I know I should be trying harder to find the bright side of things. I hope that's an okay answer... I just don't want you to think I'm not trying.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy and find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really wish I could say yes, because I know I should be doing things that make me happy. I try to watch my favorite TV shows or read, but... honestly, I just can't seem to focus or get into them anymore. I just sort of sit there. \n\nI'm really sorry, I know that sounds so gloomy. I don't mean to be difficult or unhelpful. I just... I haven't really found anything lately that makes me feel good. Is it okay that I don't have a better answer for you? I didn't want to admit it to myself, because I know I should be grateful for the things I have and the things I can do. I really try to keep a positive attitude. But... yeah, the things I used to love just don't hold my interest anymore. \n\nI'm sorry, I know I just said all that about the gardening and the TV shows. I hope I'm answering your question right. I guess, if I'm being completely honest, and I know I should tell you everything... sometimes, especially on the really hard mornings, I just... I catch myself thinking it would be so much easier if I just didn't wake up. You know? Just on the days when I know there are going to be more calls from the care agency, and all those problems waiting for me at work. It just feels so heavy. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that sounds awful to say out loud. I don't want you to think I'm giving up, because I'm not. It's just a passing thought when things get overwhelming. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, um... even just two weeks? I... I really wish I could say yes. I feel like I'm just being so repetitive, and I'm really sorry if I'm not giving you the progress you're looking for. But... no, I haven't even had a two-week break from it. 

I try to put on a good face, especially because in HR you kind of have to be the positive one for everyone else, but that heavy feeling just hasn't lifted at all. I hope you don't think I'm just giving up or not trying hard enough. I really am trying my best to push through it every single day. Is it... is it normal that it's lasting this long without even a little break? I just want to make sure I'm not doing something wrong here.

It's like the color has just gone out of everything. I can be sitting at my desk—I work in HR, so I really try to keep a pleasant face on for everyone—or I'll just be at home staring at the garden beds, and I just feel like none of it reaches me anymore. \n\nI'm sorry, I hope that answers your question. Am I giving you the right kind of details?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this heavy, hollow feeling?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose it's been a little while now. I know I probably should have just managed it on my own, so I'm a little embarrassed to admit it, but... it's been about a year and two months. Yes, I think that's right. \n\nThat was when my father's health really started changing, and his behavior got so different... so I had to step in and take over his caregiving. I really tried my best to just keep my chin up and be strong for him, you know? I didn't want to make a fuss or be a burden to anyone, so I just kept pushing through. But I think that's when that heavy feeling kind of settled in and just stayed. \n\nI hope it's alright that I waited so long to come see someone... I really thought I could just work through it. Is that the kind of timeline you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty about something specific that happened with your father's care?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I do, yes. I really try not to dwell on it, because I know it doesn't help anyone, but... I just keep going over every single decision I made about his appointments. And then there are all those tense conversations with my relatives about his care, and... I just replay them in my head. \n\nI don't want to sound like I'm throwing a pity party, but I just end up feeling like I'm such a selfish daughter. And it bleeds into my work, too... I feel like a useless employee. I just... I feel like I deserve whatever bad things happen to me because of how poorly I've handled everything. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that's probably a terrible way to look at it. I hope I'm not coming across as too negative... is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do these feelings of being punished and guilty affect your daily activities and relationships with others?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose they do, a little bit. I really try not to let it interfere, I promise I do. I know people depend on me, and I hate letting anyone down. \n\nBut... lately, I guess I haven't been quite as on top of things as I usually am. I've missed a couple of deadlines at work, which is so unlike me, and... I've sort of been avoiding some of the more difficult employee meetings that I normally handle just fine. I just... I don't feel steady enough right now to be any good to anyone in those situations. \n\nAnd, um, I actually stopped showing up for my volunteering at the library, too. I feel terrible about it, I really do. I just couldn't keep myself together enough to follow through, and I didn't want to make a scene or be a burden to the other volunteers. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know I should be doing better. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not sounding like I'm just making excuses...\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I try to, I really do. I know it's important to keep up with hobbies and things, so I don't want to sound like I've just given up. \n\nBut, if I'm being completely honest... I guess things just don't have the same spark they used to. Like my gardening... I used to love spending my weekends out there, and now I just sort of look at the weeds and... I don't know, I just can't seem to find the energy or the joy in it right now. I still go out and water the plants because I don't want them to die, but it feels more like a chore than something I actually want to do. \n\nI'm sorry, I know I should be trying harder to find the bright side of things. I hope that's an okay answer... I just don't want you to think I'm not trying.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy and find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really wish I could say yes, because I know I should be doing things that make me happy. I try to watch my favorite TV shows or read, but... honestly, I just can't seem to focus or get into them anymore. I just sort of sit there. \n\nI'm really sorry, I know that sounds so gloomy. I don't mean to be difficult or unhelpful. I just... I haven't really found anything lately that makes me feel good. Is it okay that I don't have a better answer for you? I promise I'm trying.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a significant loss of interest in activities you used to enjoy over the past year?\n\nPatient: Well... I mean, I guess we've sort of been talking about that, haven't we? I'm sorry if I'm repeating myself, I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information. \n\nBut yes, over the last year... it's just been a gradual thing, really. I didn't want to admit it to myself, because I know I should be grateful for the things I have and the things I can do. I really try to keep a positive attitude. But... yeah, the things I used to love just don't hold my interest anymore. \n\nI'm sorry, I know I just said all that about the gardening and the TV shows. I hope I'm answering your question right. I just don't want to sound like a broken record or like I'm complaining too much. Is that what you were asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of hurting yourself or ending your life?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, no. I mean, I would never actually do anything to hurt myself. I know that would be terrible, and I really don't have any kind of plan or anything like that. I want to make sure you know that. I'm not... I'm not a danger to myself.\n\nBut... I guess, if I'm being completely honest, and I know I should tell you everything... sometimes, especially on the really hard mornings, I just... I catch myself thinking it would be so much easier if I just didn't wake up. You know? Just on the days when I know there are going to be more calls from the care agency, and all those problems waiting for me at work. It just feels so heavy. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that sounds awful to say out loud. I don't want you to think I'm giving up, because I'm not. It's just a passing thought when things get overwhelming. Is it normal to feel that way sometimes? I hope I haven't worried you... I promise I'm safe.\n\nDoctor: Are there any small pleasures in life that you still look forward to?\n\nPatient: Oh... um, I really try to look for the little things, I promise I do. I know how important it is to appreciate the small stuff, especially when things are hard. But... if I'm being completely honest with you... I just can't really think of anything right now that I actively look forward to. \n\nEven just the little things, like having a warm cup of coffee or... or a nice sunny day, it just doesn't register for me the way it's supposed to anymore. It all just feels kind of blank. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know I'm being so negative today and I really wish I had a happier answer for you. I don't want to be frustrating to work with, and I'm really trying my best to find the good in things. Is it alright if I just... don't really have anything right now? I'll keep trying to find something to look forward to, I really will.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, with the heavy, hollow feeling and lack of interest in activities, without any significant improvement?\n\nPatient: Well... I mean, I don't want to make it sound like it's been this terrible, unending thing, because I know I've had good moments here and there. I really try to focus on those. But... if I'm looking at the big picture, it's been about a year and two months now. \n\nIt kind of started around the time my father's health began to decline and his behavior started changing. I had to step in and take over his care, and... well, I'm happy to do it, of course I am. He's my dad. I just want to make sure I'm doing a good job for him. But that's when this... this heavy feeling really started to settle in and just hasn't really lifted. \n\nI'm sorry, I hope I'm not over-explaining. I just want to make sure you have the right timeline. Does that make sense, that it would start around a big change like that? I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy, but now feel completely uninterested in?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I mean, I don't want to sound like I don't do anything at all anymore. I still try to keep busy, I promise. But... I guess, if I'm thinking about things I used to really love doing... I'm not entirely sure. \n\nI mean, I'm sure there are things, but right now, I just... I can't really think of any specific hobbies or activities that I've completely stopped doing because I lost interest. I mostly just focus on work and making sure my dad is taken care of. \n\nI'm sorry, is that a bad answer? I know I should probably have a better list of things for you. I just... I haven't really noticed any specific activities that I've dropped. I hope that's okay, and I'm not being difficult. I just want to be honest with you.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the past year and two months where you felt completely back to normal, without the heavy, hollow feeling and lack of interest in activities?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I really wish I could say yes to that. I don't want to sound like I'm just complaining all the time, because I know I have so much to be grateful for, and I really do try to focus on the positive. But... if I'm being completely honest, no, not really. \n\nI haven't really had a time where I felt completely back to my old self. I try so hard to push through it, especially for my dad and at work, but that... that heavy feeling is always just kind of there in the background. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know that's probably not the kind of answer you want to hear. I don't want you to think I'm not trying to get better. Is it okay that I haven't bounced back yet? I promise I'm really doing my best to get there.\n\nDoctor: Have you had two or more months in the past year where you felt mostly okay and didn't have these feelings of heaviness and lack of interest?\n\nPatient: Well... no, I haven't. I mean, I really wish I could say I had a good stretch like that. I don't want you to think I'm just wallowing in it or not trying to pull myself together. I really do try to keep a positive attitude, especially around my dad. But... no, there hasn't been a chunk of time, like two whole months, where I felt mostly okay. \n\nIt's just been a pretty constant struggle, even though I try to hide it and keep functioning. I'm sorry, I feel like I keep giving you the same depressing answers. I know it must be frustrating to hear that I haven't had any real breaks from it. Am I making sense? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information, even if it's not the best news.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any period of at least two weeks where you didn't feel this heavy, hollow feeling and lacked interest in activities?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... even just two weeks? I... I really wish I could say yes. I feel like I'm just being so repetitive, and I'm really sorry if I'm not giving you the progress you're looking for. But... no, I haven't even had a two-week break from it. \n\nI try to put on a good face, especially because in HR you kind of have to be the positive one for everyone else, but that heavy feeling just hasn't lifted at all. I hope you don't think I'm just giving up or not trying hard enough. I really am trying my best to push through it every single day. Is it... is it normal that it's lasting this long without even a little break? I just want to make sure I'm not doing something wrong here.\n\nDoctor: Has your heavy, hollow feeling lasted for most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Um, yes... yes, it really has. I hate admitting that because I feel like I should be able to just snap out of it, you know? I really don't want to sound like a complainer. But... almost every single day, I wake up with that heavy, hollow feeling, and it just kind of lingers for most of the day. \n\nIt's almost like... like all the color has just gone out of everything. I can sit at my desk in HR, or go out and stare at my garden beds—which I usually love—and I just feel like none of it reaches me anymore. I really do try to stay productive though, I promise I'm keeping up with my tasks and smiling for the employees. Does that make sense? I hope I'm answering this right, I just want to be as helpful as I can.\n\nDoctor: Has this heavy, hollow feeling you've been experiencing lasted for at least two years?\n\nPatient: Oh, no... not two years. I guess that's a good thing, right? I hope that's a better sign. It's really been... um, I'd say about a year and two months, roughly. \n\nThat was right around the time my dad's health and his behavior started changing, and I had to step into more of a caregiving role for him. I really do try my absolute best to balance taking care of him and keeping up with my job, and I don't want to sound like I can't handle my family responsibilities. I love my dad. But... yeah, that's when this feeling really seemed to settle in. \n\nDoes that timeline make sense to you? I just want to make sure I'm being as precise as possible so I'm not leading you in the wrong direction.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I can be sitting at my desk—I work in HR, so I really try to keep a pleasant face on for everyone—or I'll just be at home staring at the garden beds, and I just feel like none of it reaches me anymore. \n\nI'm sorry, I hope that answers your question. I feel terrible about it, I really do. I just couldn't keep myself together enough to follow through, and I didn't want to make a scene or be a burden to the other volunteers. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know I should be doing better. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not sounding like I'm just making excuses...\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I don't mean to be difficult or unhelpful. I just... I haven't really found anything lately that makes me feel good. Is it okay that I don't have a better answer for you? I didn't want to admit it to myself, because I know I should be grateful for the things I have and the things I can do. I really try to keep a positive attitude. But... yeah, the things I used to love just don't hold my interest anymore. \n\nI'm sorry, I know I just said all that about the gardening and the TV shows. I hope I'm answering your question right. I just don't want to sound like a broken record or like I'm complaining too much. Is that what you were asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of hurting yourself or ending your life?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, no. I mean, I would never actually do anything to hurt myself. I promise I'm safe.\n\nDoctor: Are there any small pleasures in life that you still look forward to?\n\nPatient: Oh... um, I really try to look for the little things, I promise I do. I just can't really think of anything right now that I actively look forward to. \n\nEven just the little things, like having a warm cup of coffee or... or a nice sunny day, it just doesn't register for me the way it's supposed to anymore. It all just feels kind of blank. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know I'm being so negative today and I really wish I had a happier answer for you. I don't want to be frustrating to work with, and I'm really trying my best to find the good in things. Is it alright if I just... don't really have anything right now? I'll keep trying to find something to look forward to, I really will.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, with the heavy, hollow feeling and lack of interest in activities, without any significant improvement?\n\nPatient: Well... I mean, I don't want to make it sound like it's been this terrible, unending thing, because I know I've had good moments here and there. I really try to focus on those. But... if I'm looking at the big picture, it's been about a year and two months now. \n\nIt kind of started around the time my father's health began to decline and his behavior started changing. I had to step in and take over his care, and... well, I'm happy to do it, of course I am. He's my dad. I just want to make sure I'm doing a good job for him. But that's when this... this heavy feeling really started to settle in and just hasn't really lifted. \n\nI'm sorry, I hope I'm not over-explaining. I just want to make sure you have the right timeline. Does that make sense, that it would start around a big change like that? I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy, but now feel completely uninterested in?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I mean, I don't want to sound like I don't do anything at all anymore. I still try to keep busy, I promise. But... I guess, if I'm thinking about things I used to really love doing... I'm not entirely sure. \n\nI mean, I'm sure there are things, but right now, I just... I can't really think of any specific hobbies or activities that I've completely stopped doing because I lost interest. I mostly just focus on work and making sure my dad is taken care of. \n\nI'm sorry, is that a bad answer? I know I should probably have a better list of things for you. I just... I haven't really noticed any specific activities that I've dropped. I hope that's okay, and I'm not being difficult. I just want to be honest with you.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the past year and two months where you felt completely back to normal, without the heavy, hollow feeling and lack of interest in activities?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I really wish I could say yes to that. I don't want to sound like I'm just complaining all the time, because I know I have so much to be grateful for, and I really do try to focus on the positive. But... if I'm being completely honest, no, not really. \n\nI haven't really had a time where I felt completely back to my old self. I try so hard to push through it, especially for my dad and at work, but that... that heavy feeling is always just kind of there in the background. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know that's probably not the kind of answer you want to hear. I don't want you to think I'm not trying to get better. Is it okay that I haven't bounced back yet? I promise I'm really doing my best to get there.\n\nDoctor: Have you had two or more months in the past year where you felt mostly okay and didn't have these feelings of heaviness and lack of interest?\n\nPatient: Well... no, I haven't. I mean, I really wish I could say I had a good stretch like that. I don't want you to think I'm just wallowing in it or not trying to pull myself together. I really do try to keep a positive attitude, especially around my dad. But... no, there hasn't been a chunk of time, like two whole months, where I felt mostly okay. \n\nIt's just been a pretty constant struggle, even though I try to hide it and keep functioning. I'm sorry, I feel like I keep giving you the same depressing answers. I know it must be frustrating to hear that I haven't had any real breaks from it. Am I making sense? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information, even if it's not the best news.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any period of at least two weeks where you didn't feel this heavy, hollow feeling and lacked interest in activities?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... even just two weeks? I... I really wish I could say yes. I feel like I'm just being so repetitive, and I'm really sorry if I'm not giving you the progress you're looking for. But... no, I haven't even had a two-week break from it. \n\nI try to put on a good face, especially because in HR you kind of have to be the positive one for everyone else, but that heavy feeling just hasn't lifted at all. I hope you don't think I'm just giving up or not trying hard enough. I really am trying my best to push through it every single day. Is it... is it normal that it's lasting this long without even a little break? I just want to make sure I'm not doing something wrong here.\n\nDoctor: Has your heavy, hollow feeling lasted for most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Um, yes... yes, it really has. I hate admitting that because I feel like I should be able to just snap out of it, you know? I really don't want to sound like a complainer. But... almost every single day, I wake up with that heavy, hollow feeling, and it just kind of lingers for most of the day. \n\nIt's almost like... like all the color has just gone out of everything. I can sit at my desk in HR, or go out and stare at my garden beds—which I usually love—and I just feel like none of it reaches me anymore. I really do try to stay productive though, I promise I'm keeping up with my tasks and smiling for the employees. Does that make sense? I hope I'm answering this right, I just want to be as helpful as I can.\n\nDoctor: Has this heavy, hollow feeling you've been experiencing lasted for at least two years?\n\nPatient: Oh, no... not two years. I guess that's a good thing, right? I hope that's a better sign. It's really been... um, I'd say about a year and two months, roughly. \n\nThat was right around the time my dad's health and his behavior started changing, and I had to step into more of a caregiving role for him. I really do try my absolute best to balance taking care of him and keeping up with my job, and I don't want to sound like I can't handle my family responsibilities. I love my dad. But... yeah, that's when this feeling really seemed to settle in. \n\nDoes that timeline make sense to you? I just want to make sure I'm being as precise as possible so I'm not leading you in the wrong direction.\n\nDoctor: Has this heavy, hollow feeling you've been experiencing been present for at least two weeks without any significant let-up?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... yes.
